\section*{How to use this packet}
For each source claim, compare the exact source excerpts with the expanded
PaperInterface specification. Mark up this PDF or fill the blank reviewer box in the TeX source;
return the annotated file for reconciliation into the paper-local human-review
log.

\section*{Open the interactive dashboard}
The interactive dashboard is an optional alternative to using this PDF; it
presents the same review surface rather than an additional review requirement.
After forking and cloning (or pulling) AppliedModelingLib, run the following from the
repository root. The cache command is needed only the first time in a new clone.
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
cd <your-repository-checkout>
lake exe cache get
python3 scripts/review_dashboard.py --paper GHW01DigitalGoods --serve
\end{ReviewVerbatim}
Open \url{http://127.0.0.1:8765/} in a browser and keep that terminal running
while reviewing. The dashboard records saved annotations in this paper's local
reviewer-owned review record.

\subsection*{\small\ttfamily\raggedright Applied\allowbreak{}Modeling\allowbreak{}Lib.\allowbreak{}Auction.\allowbreak{}Deterministic\allowbreak{}Offer\allowbreak{}Bid\allowbreak{}Independent}
\textbf{Declaration location:} reusable library
\paragraph{Lean declaration body}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
type:
(ℝ → Option ℝ) → Prop

value:
fun (offer : ℝ → Option ℝ) =>
  (∀ (report : ℝ), offer report = none) ∨
    ∃ (criticalPrice : ℝ),
      (∀ (report price : ℝ), offer report = some price → price = criticalPrice) ∧
        (∀ report < criticalPrice, offer report = none) ∧
          ∀ (report : ℝ), criticalPrice < report → offer report = some criticalPrice
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

value:
fun {highCount lowCount : ℕ} (bids : AppliedModelingLib.Auction.TwoValueAgent highCount lowCount → ℝ)
    (i : AppliedModelingLib.Auction.TwoValueAgent highCount lowCount) =>
  match i with
  | Sum.inl i =>
    (List.ofFn fun (j : Fin highCount) => bids (Sum.inl j)).eraseIdx ↑i ++
      List.ofFn fun (j : Fin lowCount) => bids (Sum.inr j)
  | Sum.inr i =>
    (List.ofFn fun (j : Fin highCount) => bids (Sum.inl j)) ++
      (List.ofFn fun (j : Fin lowCount) => bids (Sum.inr j)).eraseIdx ↑i
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

\subsection*{\small\ttfamily\raggedright GHW01\allowbreak{}Digital\allowbreak{}Goods.\allowbreak{}Source\allowbreak{}Definitions.\allowbreak{}directional\allowbreak{}Optimal\allowbreak{}Threshold\allowbreak{}Auction}
\textbf{Declaration location:} paper-local
\paragraph{Lean declaration body}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
type:
{Agent : Type u_1} →
  [Fintype Agent] →
    [Nonempty Agent] →
      [DecidableEq Agent] →
        (sample : Finset Agent) →
          sample.Nonempty →
            GHW01DigitalGoods.SourceDefinitions.ThresholdBoundary → AppliedModelingLib.Auction.DigitalGoodsAuction Agent

value:
fun {Agent : Type u_1} [Fintype Agent] [Nonempty Agent] [DecidableEq Agent] (sample : Finset Agent)
    (hsample : sample.Nonempty) (boundary : GHW01DigitalGoods.SourceDefinitions.ThresholdBoundary) =>
  {
    allocation := fun (bids : Agent → ℝ) (i : Agent) =>
      have price : ℝ := GHW01DigitalGoods.SourceDefinitions.optimalThresholdOnFinset sample hsample bids;
      if i ∈ sample then 0
      else
        if GHW01DigitalGoods.SourceDefinitions.accepts { price := price, boundary := boundary } (bids i) then 1 else 0,
    payment := fun (bids : Agent → ℝ) (i : Agent) =>
      have price : ℝ := GHW01DigitalGoods.SourceDefinitions.optimalThresholdOnFinset sample hsample bids;
      if i ∈ sample then 0
      else
        if GHW01DigitalGoods.SourceDefinitions.accepts { price := price, boundary := boundary } (bids i) then price
        else 0 }
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

\subsection*{\small\ttfamily\raggedright GHW01\allowbreak{}Digital\allowbreak{}Goods.\allowbreak{}Source\allowbreak{}Definitions.\allowbreak{}dual\allowbreak{}Price\allowbreak{}Random\allowbreak{}Sampling\allowbreak{}Auction}
\textbf{Declaration location:} paper-local
\paragraph{Lean declaration body}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
type:
{Agent : Type u_1} →
  [Fintype Agent] →
    [DecidableEq Agent] →
      Finset Agent →
        (Multiset ℝ → GHW01DigitalGoods.SourceDefinitions.ThresholdOffer) →
          AppliedModelingLib.Auction.DigitalGoodsAuction Agent

value:
fun {Agent : Type u_1} [Fintype Agent] [DecidableEq Agent] (sample : Finset Agent)
    (f : Multiset ℝ → GHW01DigitalGoods.SourceDefinitions.ThresholdOffer) =>
  {
    allocation := fun (bids : Agent → ℝ) (i : Agent) =>
      have offer : GHW01DigitalGoods.SourceDefinitions.ThresholdOffer :=
        if i ∈ sample then f (GHW01DigitalGoods.SourceDefinitions.sampleBidMultiset (Finset.univ \ sample) bids)
        else f (GHW01DigitalGoods.SourceDefinitions.sampleBidMultiset sample bids);
      if GHW01DigitalGoods.SourceDefinitions.accepts offer (bids i) then 1 else 0,
    payment := fun (bids : Agent → ℝ) (i : Agent) =>
      have offer : GHW01DigitalGoods.SourceDefinitions.ThresholdOffer :=
        if i ∈ sample then f (GHW01DigitalGoods.SourceDefinitions.sampleBidMultiset (Finset.univ \ sample) bids)
        else f (GHW01DigitalGoods.SourceDefinitions.sampleBidMultiset sample bids);
      if GHW01DigitalGoods.SourceDefinitions.accepts offer (bids i) then offer.price else 0 }
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

\begin{itemize}\raggedright
\item \hyperlink{governing-declaration-7d5372964da104c4}{Applied\allowbreak{}Modeling\allowbreak{}Lib.\allowbreak{}Auction.\allowbreak{}Digital\allowbreak{}Goods\allowbreak{}Auction}

\item \hyperlink{governing-declaration-b26c3ea1f1b28e35}{GHW01\allowbreak{}Digital\allowbreak{}Goods.\allowbreak{}Source\allowbreak{}Definitions.\allowbreak{}sample\allowbreak{}Bid\allowbreak{}Multiset}
\end{itemize}

\subsection*{\small\ttfamily\raggedright GHW01\allowbreak{}Digital\allowbreak{}Goods.\allowbreak{}Source\allowbreak{}Definitions.\allowbreak{}random\allowbreak{}Sampling\allowbreak{}Auction}
\textbf{Declaration location:} paper-local
\paragraph{Lean declaration body}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
type:
{Agent : Type u_1} →
  [DecidableEq Agent] →
    Finset Agent →
      (Multiset ℝ → GHW01DigitalGoods.SourceDefinitions.ThresholdOffer) →
        AppliedModelingLib.Auction.DigitalGoodsAuction Agent

value:
fun {Agent : Type u_1} [DecidableEq Agent] (sample : Finset Agent)
    (f : Multiset ℝ → GHW01DigitalGoods.SourceDefinitions.ThresholdOffer) =>
  {
    allocation := fun (bids : Agent → ℝ) (i : Agent) =>
      if i ∈ sample then 0
      else
        if
            GHW01DigitalGoods.SourceDefinitions.accepts
              (f (GHW01DigitalGoods.SourceDefinitions.sampleBidMultiset sample bids)) (bids i) then
          1
        else 0,
    payment := fun (bids : Agent → ℝ) (i : Agent) =>
      if i ∈ sample then 0
      else
        if
            GHW01DigitalGoods.SourceDefinitions.accepts
              (f (GHW01DigitalGoods.SourceDefinitions.sampleBidMultiset sample bids)) (bids i) then
          (f (GHW01DigitalGoods.SourceDefinitions.sampleBidMultiset sample bids)).price
        else 0 }
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

fun (rule : GHW01DigitalGoods.PaperBidIndependentBoundaryRule) (others : Multiset ℝ) (bid : ℝ) =>
  if rule.acceptsEquality others = true then if rule.price others ≤ bid then rule.price others else 0
  else if rule.price others < bid then rule.price others else 0
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

\subsection*{\small\ttfamily\raggedright GHW01\allowbreak{}Digital\allowbreak{}Goods.\allowbreak{}random\allowbreak{}Sampling\allowbreak{}Optimal\allowbreak{}Threshold\allowbreak{}Auction}
\textbf{Declaration location:} paper-local
\paragraph{Lean declaration body}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
type:
{Agent : Type u_1} →
  [Fintype Agent] →
    [Nonempty Agent] →
      [DecidableEq Agent] → (Agent → Bool) → Bool → ℕ → AppliedModelingLib.Auction.DigitalGoodsAuction Agent

value:
fun {Agent : Type u_1} [Fintype Agent] [Nonempty Agent] [DecidableEq Agent] (side : Agent → Bool) (sampleSide : Bool)
    (minWinners : ℕ) =>
  {
    allocation := fun (bids : Agent → ℝ) (i : Agent) =>
      if side i = sampleSide then 0
      else
        if
            AppliedModelingLib.Auction.finiteCandidateOfferPrice
                (AppliedModelingLib.Auction.restrictBidsBySide side sampleSide bids) minWinners ≤
              bids i then
          1
        else 0,
    payment := fun (bids : Agent → ℝ) (i : Agent) =>
      if side i = sampleSide then 0
      else
        if
            AppliedModelingLib.Auction.finiteCandidateOfferPrice
                (AppliedModelingLib.Auction.restrictBidsBySide side sampleSide bids) minWinners ≤
              bids i then
          AppliedModelingLib.Auction.finiteCandidateOfferPrice
            (AppliedModelingLib.Auction.restrictBidsBySide side sampleSide bids) minWinners
        else 0 }
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
type:
{Agent : Type u_1} →
  [inst : Fintype Agent] →
    [Nonempty Agent] →
      [inst_2 : LinearOrder Agent] →
        (sample : Finset Agent) →
          sample.Nonempty →
            (Finset.univ \ sample).Nonempty →
              GHW01DigitalGoods.SourceDefinitions.SupplyCapacity → AppliedModelingLib.Auction.DigitalGoodsAuction Agent

value:
fun {Agent : Type u_1} [Fintype Agent] [Nonempty Agent] [LinearOrder Agent] (sample : Finset Agent)
    (hsample : sample.Nonempty) (hmarket : (Finset.univ \ sample).Nonempty)
    (halfCapacity : GHW01DigitalGoods.SourceDefinitions.SupplyCapacity) =>
  have side : Agent → Bool := GHW01DigitalGoods.sampledSideAssignment sample;
  have left : AppliedModelingLib.Auction.DigitalGoodsAuction Agent :=
    GHW01DigitalGoods.boundedCappedThresholdAuction side false
      (fun (bids : Agent → ℝ) =>
        GHW01DigitalGoods.SourceDefinitions.boundedOptimalThresholdOnFinset sample hsample bids halfCapacity)
      halfCapacity;
  have right : AppliedModelingLib.Auction.DigitalGoodsAuction Agent :=
    GHW01DigitalGoods.boundedCappedThresholdAuction side true
      (fun (bids : Agent → ℝ) =>
        GHW01DigitalGoods.SourceDefinitions.boundedOptimalThresholdOnFinset (Finset.univ \ sample) hmarket bids
          halfCapacity)
      halfCapacity;
  {
    allocation := fun (bids : Agent → ℝ) (i : Agent) =>
      if side i = false then left.allocation bids i else right.allocation bids i,
    payment := fun (bids : Agent → ℝ) (i : Agent) =>
      if side i = false then left.payment bids i else right.payment bids i }
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

        ↑(AppliedModelingLib.Auction.twoValueErasedBidList highValue (highCount - 1) lowCount) ↑highValue +

\paragraph{Verbatim paper-source connection}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
We say that an auction is truthful if bidding ui is
a dominant strategy for bidder i. More specifically,
let a bidder’s profit be the difference between the bidder’s utility value and the price the bidder pays if they
win the auction, or zero if they lose. Then an auction
is truthful if a bidder’s profit (or expected profit, for
randomized auctions), as a function of the bidder’s
bid, is maximized at the bidder’s utility value, for
any fixed values of the other bidders’ bids. Truthful
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

We view auctions as algorithms for deciding which
input bids to fill, and at what price. As with any algorithm, one needs to address the issues of correctness,
efficiency, and performance. In the context of this
paper, an auction is correct if the auction is truthful
and fills each winning bid at or below the bid value.
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

To get a better understanding of how the optimal single-price auction works, we define the optimal
threshold function, opt(B). This function on a set of
bids B returns the fixed price at which items should
be sold to achieve revenue F . In the optimal singleprice auction, all bid values that are at least opt(B)
will be satisfied at price opt(B). All other bids will
be rejected. That is,

5

Generalized Truthful Auction Mechanisms

By making observations about auctions that encourage utility value bids, in particular the Vickrey auction, we can design general auction mechanisms. We
present two general auction mechanisms that facilitate the design of truthful auctions. The first, the bidindependent auction mechanism, is based on the observation that the price that a bid is satisfied at must
be independent from that bid’s actual value. The
second, the random sampling auction mechanism, is
based on the observation that rejected bids can be
used to set prices for bids that are to be satisfied.

opt(B) = argmaxbi ∈B bi · (n − i + 1).
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

on randomized sampling. We select a subset B ′ of B
at random, independent of the bid values. Let m be
the size of B ′ . We then compute a function on these
sampled bids, f (B ′ ), and use this as a threshold value
for the n−m bids in the non-sample, B\B ′ . Note that
this auction mechanism is inherently single-price.
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

loss due to the rejected bids in the sample; however,
it is at the expense of making the auction dual-price.
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

\paragraph{Verbatim paper-source connection}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
We say that an auction is truthful if bidding ui is
a dominant strategy for bidder i. More specifically,
let a bidder’s profit be the difference between the bidder’s utility value and the price the bidder pays if they
win the auction, or zero if they lose. Then an auction
is truthful if a bidder’s profit (or expected profit, for
randomized auctions), as a function of the bidder’s
bid, is maximized at the bidder’s utility value, for
any fixed values of the other bidders’ bids. Truthful
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

\paragraph{Verbatim paper-source connection}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
We view auctions as algorithms for deciding which
input bids to fill, and at what price. As with any algorithm, one needs to address the issues of correctness,
efficiency, and performance. In the context of this
paper, an auction is correct if the auction is truthful
and fills each winning bid at or below the bid value.
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

\paragraph{Verbatim paper-source connection}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
Analogous to on-line algorithm theory, we express
auction performance relative to that for optimal untruthful auctions, as ratios R/T or R/F . However,
we solve a maximization problem while on-line algorithms solve minimization problems. Thus, positive
results, which are lower bounds on R/T or R/F , are
expressed using “Ω”. Impossibility results, which are
upper bounds on R/T or R/F for any auction in a
certain class, are expressed using “O”.
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

items to consumers who each want a single item and
the items are available in unlimited supply. By unlimited supply we mean that either the seller has at
least as many items as there are consumers, or that
the seller can reproduce items on demand at negligible marginal cost. Of particular interest are digital items such as downloadable audio files and payper-view movies. With unlimited supply, consumer
utilities, the maximum amounts that consumers are
willing to pay for the item, are the sole factor determining sale prices. The seller’s goal is to maximize
their total revenue.
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
Consider the problem of selling a number of identical
items to consumers who each want a single item and
the items are available in unlimited supply. By unlimited supply we mean that either the seller has at
least as many items as there are consumers, or that
the seller can reproduce items on demand at negligible marginal cost. Of particular interest are digital items such as downloadable audio files and payper-view movies. With unlimited supply, consumer
utilities, the maximum amounts that consumers are
willing to pay for the item, are the sole factor determining sale prices. The seller’s goal is to maximize
their total revenue.

of bids without bidder i’s bid. A bid-independent

\subsection*{\small\ttfamily\raggedright GHW01\allowbreak{}Digital\allowbreak{}Goods.\allowbreak{}Source\allowbreak{}Definitions.\allowbreak{}exact\allowbreak{}Half\allowbreak{}Optimal\allowbreak{}Threshold\allowbreak{}Auction}
\noindent\textbf{Paper-local declaration:}\par

\textbf{Source locator:} {\footnotesize\raggedright cited publication, lines 455-\allowbreak{}465\par}
\paragraph{Verbatim paper-source connection}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
The random sampling optimal threshold auction
takes f = opt, the optimal threshold function, in
the random sampling auction. Intuitively we use the
sample to get an idea for a good threshold value,
then we apply that threshold to the remaining bids.
The auction samples m = n/2 bids at random, computes opt of this sample, and uses this value as a
threshold for the non-sample, accepting all bids above
this threshold at the threshold value. In this section
we show that the expected revenue of this auction is
within a constant factor of F , assuming F /h is not
too small.
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

5

Generalized Truthful Auction Mechanisms

By making observations about auctions that encourage utility value bids, in particular the Vickrey auction, we can design general auction mechanisms. We
present two general auction mechanisms that facilitate the design of truthful auctions. The first, the bidindependent auction mechanism, is based on the observation that the price that a bid is satisfied at must
be independent from that bid’s actual value. The
second, the random sampling auction mechanism, is
based on the observation that rejected bids can be
used to set prices for bids that are to be satisfied.

opt(B) = argmaxbi ∈B bi · (n − i + 1).
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

\paragraph{Verbatim paper-source connection}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
Analogous to on-line algorithm theory, we express
auction performance relative to that for optimal untruthful auctions, as ratios R/T or R/F . However,
we solve a maximization problem while on-line algorithms solve minimization problems. Thus, positive
results, which are lower bounds on R/T or R/F , are
expressed using “Ω”. Impossibility results, which are
upper bounds on R/T or R/F for any auction in a
certain class, are expressed using “O”.
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

\section*{Source claims}
\subsection*{Review row 1}
\textbf{Source locator:} {\footnotesize\raggedright cited publication, lines 359-\allowbreak{}370\par}
\paragraph{Verbatim source input}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
Theorem 4.1 F ≥ T /(2 log h).
Proof. Divide the bids into log h bins by partitioning the bids at values of powers of two. Thus, each
bid is less than a factor of two from any other bid
in the same bin. Since the sum of the bids is T and
there are log h bins, then some bin has a sum of at
least T / log h. Note that the lowest bid in this bin is
at least half of any other bid in the bin. If the optimal single-price auction chose, as its selling price, the
price of the lowest bid in this bin, then the contribution of each bid in this bin to the revenue is at least
half of the bid’s value. Since the bin sums to more
than T / log h, this means that the revenue is greater
than T /(2 log h). Thus the optimal fixed pricing can
always achieve a revenue of at least T /(2 log h).
\end{ReviewVerbatim}


\paragraph{Formalized review target}
The archival source above is not asserted equivalent to this formalized target.
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
Let h >= 2. For a nonempty finite bid profile whose values all lie in [1, h], its total value T satisfies T <= (2 log_2 h) F, where F is the optimal fixed-price revenue.
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

\textbf{Archival source anchor:} {\footnotesize\raggedright cited publication, lines 359-\allowbreak{}370\par}

\section*{Review row 2}
\textbf{Source locator:} {\footnotesize\raggedright cited publication, lines 301-\allowbreak{}307\par}
\paragraph{Verbatim source input}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
Corollary 4.2 F ≥ T /(4 log n).
Proof. Let p be the optimal price; clearly p ≥ h/n.
If one drops all bids with values below p, F does not
change and T decreases by at most a factor of two.
After the bids are dropped, the ratio of the highest
and the lowest bid values is at most n, yielding the
desired result.
\end{ReviewVerbatim}


\paragraph{Formalized review target}
The archival source above is not asserted equivalent to this formalized target.
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
Let n >= 2 be the number of bidders. For a nonempty finite nonnegative bid profile with positive maximum value h and every value at most h, T <= (4 log_2 n) F, where F is the optimal fixed-price revenue.
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

[Next verbatim source excerpt]

To enable analysis of auction revenue we define several properties of an input set of bids. As stated in
the introduction, T is the sum of all of the bids. An
equivalent definition of T is the revenue due to the
optimal multi-price untruthful auction, the one that
satisfies all bids at their value. The revenue for optimal fixed pricing is F . Note that F can also be
interpreted as the revenue due to the optimal singleprice untruthful auction. More discussion of F and
its relation to the optimal single-price untruthful auction is given in Section 4. Other properties that we
use in analysis are ell, the smallest bid value, and h,
the highest bid value. Because bids can be arbitrarily scaled, we assume, without loss of generality, that
ell = 1, in which case h is really the ratio of the highest
bid to the lowest bid.
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
For a randomized offer auction on a finite bidder set with nonnegative bid values and nonnegative real offer prices, with arbitrary bidderwise probability laws, suppose that whenever b_i <= b_j and x <= b_i, the probability that bidder i wins at a price at most x is at most the corresponding probability for bidder j. Then its expected revenue on every bid vector is at most the optimal fixed-price revenue F of that vector.
\end{ReviewVerbatim}

\section*{Review row 11}
\textbf{Source locator:} {\footnotesize\raggedright cited publication, lines 1105-\allowbreak{}1128\par}
\paragraph{Verbatim source input}
\begin{ReviewVerbatim}
Lemma 9.2 Any truthful deterministic auction is
bid-independent.
Proof. Let Bi = B \ {i} as before, and let Bix be
the set of bids with bi replaced with value x. Let
A be a truthful deterministic auction. Let A(B) denote the result of running A on set B and let Ai (B)
denote the result for bidder i (∞ if i is rejected).
Consider function g defined as g(x) = Ai (Bix ). Here
g is clearly a function of A and Bi . We show that
g is ∞ everywhere except for some region (v, ∞),
[v, ∞), or {} where it has value v. This would imply that A is bid-independent. If g(x) = ∞ for all x
then we have the empty interval, {}. Otherwise, let
b = inf{x : g(x) [U+0016 control character]= ∞} and let v = minx g(x). We
now show that b = v and for all b′ > b, g(b′ ) = v.
1. v = b
First we show that v ≤ b. This is simple to see
because g(x) is defined so that either g(x) is ∞
(and a bid value of x will lose) or g(x) ≤ x (and
a bid of value x will win at price g(x) which is
necessarily at most x).
Now we show that v ≥ b. Assume for a contradiction that there exists a b′ such that v < b′ < b.
This auction is not truthful because a bidder
with utility value b′ would be better off bidding
greater than b, win the aucion and pay price v
which is less than b′ . Thus, v = b.
\end{ReviewVerbatim}
